I've actually been cutting people off a lot more lately. Like... It just pulls me right off whatever I’m doing before I can even stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Especially when I'm stuck in meetings. I'm usually bouncing my knee, or clicking a pen, or just... shifting around in my chair a lot. It's actually becoming a bit of an issue because people have started giving me these looks. They think I'm trying to rush them along, even when I'm really just trying to sit there and listen to what they're saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I run a business, so there's always the usual stress that comes with the job, but... I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried. It's not like my mind is spinning with bad thoughts or anything. It's really just the physical restlessness and getting sidetracked like I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I've actually been cutting people off a lot more lately. Like... I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or at work, I'll end up finishing an employee's thought before they're even done talking. I don't really mean to do it, but I can definitely tell it rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I interrupt, like I said, but it's more about jumping in with my own thoughts. I don't really sit there guessing what they're going to ask and blurting out an answer before they finish. It's just... I get impatient and talk over them. But not blurting out answers, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, nobody likes waiting in a long line, but I can stand there just fine. I usually just pull out my phone and check work emails or something. And as for waiting to speak... I know I just said I interrupt people, but it's not because I'm sitting there agonizing over waiting my turn. I just kind of jump in without thinking. But I don't have an actual problem waiting if I know I need to.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or insert yourself into their activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that goes back to what I mentioned a minute ago. I definitely do that. Like I said, I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or I'll step in and finish an employee's thought at work. I guess that is intruding, really. I don't mean to take over, but I do just kind of insert myself into whatever they're doing or saying, and... yeah, I can definitely tell it rubs them the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, interrupting others, and intruding on their activities are causing problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. Like when family plans don't go the way they should because I just take over. And at work, I can definitely tell I'm losing people's patience. When we're in meetings, or I'm trying to have mentoring conversations with my employees, and I keep stepping on their toes... it causes friction. So, I'm still getting things done, but yeah, it's causing problems with the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these problems with restlessness and interrupting others in all areas of your life, or are they mainly happening in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's pretty much everywhere. Like I was just saying, it happens at the office with my team, and it happens at home with my wife. I don't really... I haven't noticed it being tied to just one specific situation. But as far as my own happiness goes, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without much consideration for the consequences?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I do that, no. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what really made me come in is that for the past year or so, I've noticed the smallest things keep throwing me off track. It could be just a phone buzz, or somebody walking past my office, or... even a random thought about my in-laws. It just pulls me right off whatever I’m doing before I can even stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Especially when I'm stuck in meetings. I'm usually bouncing my knee, or clicking a pen, or just... shifting around in my chair a lot. It's actually becoming a bit of an issue because people have started giving me these looks. They think I'm trying to rush them along, even when I'm really just trying to sit there and listen to what they're saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I run a business, so there's always the usual stress that comes with the job, but... I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried. It's not like my mind is spinning with bad thoughts or anything. It's really just the physical restlessness and getting sidetracked like I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I've actually been cutting people off a lot more lately. Like... I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or at work, I'll end up finishing an employee's thought before they're even done talking. I don't really mean to do it, but I can definitely tell it rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I interrupt, like I said, but it's more about jumping in with my own thoughts. I don't really sit there guessing what they're going to ask and blurting out an answer before they finish. It's just... I get impatient and talk over them. But not blurting out answers, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, nobody likes waiting in a long line, but I can stand there just fine. I usually just pull out my phone and check work emails or something. And as for waiting to speak... I know I just said I interrupt people, but it's not because I'm sitting there agonizing over waiting my turn. I just kind of jump in without thinking. But I don't have an actual problem waiting if I know I need to.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or insert yourself into their activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that goes back to what I mentioned a minute ago. I definitely do that. Like I said, I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or I'll step in and finish an employee's thought at work. I guess that is intruding, really. I don't mean to take over, but I do just kind of insert myself into whatever they're doing or saying, and... yeah, I can definitely tell it rubs them the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, interrupting others, and intruding on their activities are causing problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. I mean, I’m still keeping up overall, so it's not like the business is falling apart or anything. But... it is starting to cost me trust at home. Like when family plans don't go the way they should because I just take over. And at work, I can definitely tell I'm losing people's patience. When we're in meetings, or I'm trying to have mentoring conversations with my employees, and I keep stepping on their toes... it causes friction. So, I'm still getting things done, but yeah, it's causing problems with the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these problems with restlessness and interrupting others in all areas of your life, or are they mainly happening in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's pretty much everywhere. Like I was just saying, it happens at the office with my team, and it happens at home with my wife. I don't really... I haven't noticed it being tied to just one specific situation. It's just kind of what I do whenever I'm around people or in a conversation. It's just... you know, a habit I need to get a better handle on so I stop annoying the people I care about. But as far as my own happiness goes, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without much consideration for the consequences?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I do that, no. I built a company, so I definitely have to weigh consequences and plan things out. It's more just the day-to-day conversation stuff where I jump the gun, not my actual actions or choices.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're alone, like suddenly changing what you're working on or switching tasks without finishing the first one?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what really made me come in is that for the past year or so, I've noticed the smallest things keep throwing me off track. It could be just a phone buzz, or somebody walking past my office, or... even a random thought about my in-laws. It just pulls me right off whatever I’m doing before I can even stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Especially when I'm stuck in meetings. I'm usually bouncing my knee, or clicking a pen, or just... shifting around in my chair a lot. It's actually becoming a bit of an issue because people have started giving me these looks. They think I'm trying to rush them along, even when I'm really just trying to sit there and listen to what they're saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I run a business, so there's always the usual stress that comes with the job, but... I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried. It's not like my mind is spinning with bad thoughts or anything. It's really just the physical restlessness and getting sidetracked like I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I've actually been cutting people off a lot more lately. Like... I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or at work, I'll end up finishing an employee's thought before they're even done talking. I don't really mean to do it, but I can definitely tell it rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I interrupt, like I said, but it's more about jumping in with my own thoughts. I don't really sit there guessing what they're going to ask and blurting out an answer before they finish. It's just... I get impatient and talk over them. But not blurting out answers, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, nobody likes waiting in a long line, but I can stand there just fine. I usually just pull out my phone and check work emails or something. And as for waiting to speak... I know I just said I interrupt people, but it's not because I'm sitting there agonizing over waiting my turn. I just kind of jump in without thinking. But I don't have an actual problem waiting if I know I need to.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or insert yourself into their activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that goes back to what I mentioned a minute ago. I definitely do that. Like I said, I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or I'll step in and finish an employee's thought at work. I guess that is intruding, really. I don't mean to take over, but I do just kind of insert myself into whatever they're doing or saying, and... yeah, I can definitely tell it rubs them the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, interrupting others, and intruding on their activities are causing problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. I mean, I’m still keeping up overall, so it's not like the business is falling apart or anything. But... it is starting to cost me trust at home. Like when family plans don't go the way they should because I just take over. And at work, I can definitely tell I'm losing people's patience. When we're in meetings, or I'm trying to have mentoring conversations with my employees, and I keep stepping on their toes... it causes friction. So, I'm still getting things done, but yeah, it's causing problems with the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these problems with restlessness and interrupting others in all areas of your life, or are they mainly happening in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's pretty much everywhere. Like I was just saying, it happens at the office with my team, and it happens at home with my wife. I don't really... I haven't noticed it being tied to just one specific situation. It's just kind of what I do whenever I'm around people or in a conversation. So yeah, I guess it's pretty across the board.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been this way, or did these behaviors start more recently in your life?\n\nPatient: I've pretty much always been this way. It's not a recent thing. Actually, looking back, I was like this when I was a kid, too. I remember my teachers were always moving my seat around because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking. And I was always blurting things out before they even finished asking a question. So, yeah, it's been going on since I was young.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these behaviors are affecting your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so, no. I mean, I'm generally a pretty happy guy. It's definitely frustrating sometimes when it causes that friction I mentioned, but... I wouldn't say it's dragging down my overall well-being or anything. \n\nI've built a good business, I have a good life. It's just... you know, a habit I need to get a better handle on so I stop annoying the people I care about. But as far as my own happiness goes, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without much consideration for the consequences?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I do that, no. I mean, I might interrupt someone or get antsy during a meeting, but I'm not out there making big, reckless decisions or doing things without thinking about what's going to happen. I built a company, so I definitely have to weigh consequences and plan things out. It's more just the day-to-day conversation stuff where I jump the gun, not my actual actions or choices.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're alone, like suddenly changing what you're working on or switching tasks without finishing the first one?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I'm working by myself, I usually stick to what I'm doing. I mean, I might get up and pace around for a minute if I'm feeling restless, but I don't just abandon a project halfway through to start something completely different. \n\nRunning the business, I kind of have to finish what I start. So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with jumping between tasks or leaving things unfinished like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do. Like, I can sit down to look over a planning document, or get into a meeting with one of my managers, and I'll be totally locked in for the first few minutes. But then... pretty soon I realize my mind drifted and I just missed half of what was said. I end up having to stop them and ask them to run it back for me. So, yeah, keeping that focus going is definitely an issue.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your ability to complete tasks or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: I mean, I’m still keeping up overall. I get the big things done. But... it is starting to cause some friction. \n\nIt’s starting to cost me trust at home, and I can definitely see it wearing on the patience of people at work. Like when meetings or mentoring conversations with my team don’t go the way they should because I missed something, or when family plans get messed up. So, I wouldn't say I'm failing to achieve my goals, but the way I'm operating is definitely putting a strain on the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or upset by the problems your behaviors are causing in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say overwhelmed, no. I mean... it's frustrating, sure. Nobody likes dealing with friction at home or at the office. But I'm not really upset by it. It's just a problem that needs fixing, you know? I'm pretty used to handling issues and putting out fires, so I don't let it get to me too much. I just want to figure out how to sort it out so things run smoother.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble maintaining friendships due to your behaviors?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my friendships are pretty solid. I don't get a ton of time to hang out anyway, between running the business and family stuff, but when I do, it's fine. The guys I'm friends with are used to how I am, so if I get a little restless or talk over them sometimes, they don't make a big deal out of it. So, no, I haven't noticed any real issues keeping friends.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what really made me come in is that for the past year or so, I've noticed the smallest things keep throwing me off track. It could be just a phone buzz, or somebody walking past my office, or... even a random thought about my in-laws. It just pulls me right off whatever I’m doing before I can even stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Especially when I'm stuck in meetings. I'm usually bouncing my knee, or clicking a pen, or just... shifting around in my chair a lot. It's actually becoming a bit of an issue because people have started giving me these looks. They think I'm trying to rush them along, even when I'm really just trying to sit there and listen to what they're saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I run a business, so there's always the usual stress that comes with the job, but... I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried. It's not like my mind is spinning with bad thoughts or anything. It's really just the physical restlessness and getting sidetracked like I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I've actually been cutting people off a lot more lately. Like... I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or at work, I'll end up finishing an employee's thought before they're even done talking. I don't really mean to do it, but I can definitely tell it rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I interrupt, like I said, but it's more about jumping in with my own thoughts. I don't really sit there guessing what they're going to ask and blurting out an answer before they finish. It's just... I get impatient and talk over them. But not blurting out answers, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, nobody likes waiting in a long line, but I can stand there just fine. I usually just pull out my phone and check work emails or something. And as for waiting to speak... I know I just said I interrupt people, but it's not because I'm sitting there agonizing over waiting my turn. I just kind of jump in without thinking. But I don't have an actual problem waiting if I know I need to.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or insert yourself into their activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that goes back to what I mentioned a minute ago. I definitely do that. Like I said, I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or I'll step in and finish an employee's thought at work. I guess that is intruding, really. I don't mean to take over, but I do just kind of insert myself into whatever they're doing or saying, and... yeah, I can definitely tell it rubs them the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, interrupting others, and intruding on their activities are causing problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. I mean, I’m still keeping up overall, so it's not like the business is falling apart or anything. But... it is starting to cost me trust at home. Like when family plans don't go the way they should because I just take over. And at work, I can definitely tell I'm losing people's patience. When we're in meetings, or I'm trying to have mentoring conversations with my employees, and I keep stepping on their toes... it causes friction. So, I'm still getting things done, but yeah, it's causing problems with the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these problems with restlessness and interrupting others in all areas of your life, or are they mainly happening in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's pretty much everywhere. Like I was just saying, it happens at the office with my team, and it happens at home with my wife. I don't really... I haven't noticed it being tied to just one specific situation. It's just kind of what I do whenever I'm around people or in a conversation. So yeah, I guess it's pretty across the board.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been this way, or did these behaviors start more recently in your life?\n\nPatient: I've pretty much always been this way. It's not a recent thing. Actually, looking back, I was like this when I was a kid, too. I remember my teachers were always moving my seat around because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking. And I was always blurting things out before they even finished asking a question. So, yeah, it's been going on since I was young.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these behaviors are affecting your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so, no. I mean, I'm generally a pretty happy guy. It's definitely frustrating sometimes when it causes that friction I mentioned, but... I wouldn't say it's dragging down my overall well-being or anything. \n\nI've built a good business, I have a good life. It's just... you know, a habit I need to get a better handle on so I stop annoying the people I care about. But as far as my own happiness goes, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without much consideration for the consequences?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I do that, no. I mean, I might interrupt someone or get antsy during a meeting, but I'm not out there making big, reckless decisions or doing things without thinking about what's going to happen. I built a company, so I definitely have to weigh consequences and plan things out. It's more just the day-to-day conversation stuff where I jump the gun, not my actual actions or choices.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're alone, like suddenly changing what you're working on or switching tasks without finishing the first one?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I'm working by myself, I usually stick to what I'm doing. I mean, I might get up and pace around for a minute if I'm feeling restless, but I don't just abandon a project halfway through to start something completely different. \n\nRunning the business, I kind of have to finish what I start. So, no, I wouldn't say I have a problem with jumping between tasks or leaving things unfinished like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I actually do. Like, I can sit down to look over a planning document, or get into a meeting with one of my managers, and I'll be totally locked in for the first few minutes. But then... pretty soon I realize my mind drifted and I just missed half of what was said. I end up having to stop them and ask them to run it back for me. So, yeah, keeping that focus going is definitely an issue.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms interfering with your ability to complete tasks or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: I mean, I’m still keeping up overall. I get the big things done. But... it is starting to cause some friction. \n\nIt’s starting to cost me trust at home, and I can definitely see it wearing on the patience of people at work. Like when meetings or mentoring conversations with my team don’t go the way they should because I missed something, or when family plans get messed up. So, I wouldn't say I'm failing to achieve my goals, but the way I'm operating is definitely putting a strain on the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or upset by the problems your behaviors are causing in your relationships?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say overwhelmed, no. I mean... it's frustrating, sure. Nobody likes dealing with friction at home or at the office. But I'm not really upset by it. It's just a problem that needs fixing, you know? I'm pretty used to handling issues and putting out fires, so I don't let it get to me too much. I just want to figure out how to sort it out so things run smoother.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble maintaining friendships due to your behaviors?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my friendships are pretty solid. I don't get a ton of time to hang out anyway, between running the business and family stuff, but when I do, it's fine. The guys I'm friends with are used to how I am, so if I get a little restless or talk over them sometimes, they don't make a big deal out of it. So, no, I haven't noticed any real issues keeping friends.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms significantly interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word significantly, no. Like I said before, I'm still running the business and getting the big stuff done. It’s more of an annoyance... a drag on efficiency, I guess. It causes those bumps with my family or my team, but it doesn't stop me from actually living my life or doing my job. So, no, I wouldn't call it a significant interference. It's just something I need to tune up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It could be just a phone buzz, or somebody walking past my office, or... even a random thought about my in-laws. It just pulls me right off whatever I’m doing before I can even stop it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Especially when I'm stuck in meetings. I'm usually bouncing my knee, or clicking a pen, or just... shifting around in my chair a lot. It's actually becoming a bit of an issue because people have started giving me these looks. They think I'm trying to rush them along, even when I'm really just trying to sit there and listen to what they're saying.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I run a business, so there's always the usual stress that comes with the job, but... I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried. It's not like my mind is spinning with bad thoughts or anything. It's really just the physical restlessness and getting sidetracked like I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I've actually been cutting people off a lot more lately. Like... I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or at work, I'll end up finishing an employee's thought before they're even done talking. I don't really mean to do it, but I can definitely tell it rubs people the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurt out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I do that. I mean, I interrupt, like I said, but it's more about jumping in with my own thoughts. I don't really sit there guessing what they're going to ask and blurting out an answer before they finish. It's just... I get impatient and talk over them. But not blurting out answers, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when waiting to speak?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, nobody likes waiting in a long line, but I can stand there just fine. I usually just pull out my phone and check work emails or something. And as for waiting to speak... I know I just said I interrupt people, but it's not because I'm sitting there agonizing over waiting my turn. I just kind of jump in without thinking. But I don't have an actual problem waiting if I know I need to.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or insert yourself into their activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think that goes back to what I mentioned a minute ago. I definitely do that. Like I said, I'll just jump right into my wife's conversations with her parents, or I'll step in and finish an employee's thought at work. I guess that is intruding, really. I don't mean to take over, but I do just kind of insert myself into whatever they're doing or saying, and... yeah, I can definitely tell it rubs them the wrong way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your difficulties with restlessness, interrupting others, and intruding on their activities are causing problems for you at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it is. I mean, I’m still keeping up overall, so it's not like the business is falling apart or anything. But... it is starting to cost me trust at home. Like when family plans don't go the way they should because I just take over. And at work, I can definitely tell I'm losing people's patience. When we're in meetings, or I'm trying to have mentoring conversations with my employees, and I keep stepping on their toes... it causes friction. So, I'm still getting things done, but yeah, it's causing problems with the people around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these problems with restlessness and interrupting others in all areas of your life, or are they mainly happening in specific situations?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's pretty much everywhere. Like I was just saying, it happens at the office with my team, and it happens at home with my wife. I don't really... I haven't noticed it being tied to just one specific situation. It's just kind of what I do whenever I'm around people or in a conversation. I mean, I might interrupt someone or get antsy during a meeting, but I'm not out there making big, reckless decisions or doing things without thinking about what's going to happen. I built a company, so I definitely have to weigh consequences and plan things out. It's more just the day-to-day conversation stuff where I jump the gun, not my actual actions or choices.\n\nDoctor: Do you often do things on impulse when you're alone, like suddenly changing what you're working on or switching tasks without finishing the first one?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. When I'm working by myself, I usually stick to what I'm doing. It's not a recent thing. Actually, looking back, I was like this when I was a kid, too. I remember my teachers were always moving my seat around because I talked way too much, or I'd just get up without asking. And I was always blurting things out before they even finished asking a question. So, yeah, it's been going on since I was young.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these behaviors are affecting your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so, no. I mean, I'm generally a pretty happy guy. It's definitely frustrating sometimes when it causes that friction I mentioned, but... I wouldn't say it's dragging down my overall well-being or anything. \n\nI've built a good business, I have a good life. I built a company, so I definitely have to weigh consequences and plan things out. I get the big things done. But... it is starting to cause some friction. \n\nIt’s starting to cost me trust at home, and I can definitely see it wearing on the patience of people at work. The guys I'm friends with are used to how I am, so if I get a little restless or talk over them sometimes, they don't make a big deal out of it. So, no, I haven't noticed any real issues keeping friends.\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms significantly interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't use the word significantly, no.
